\section*{Second Order Derivatives 67\quad\small\texttt{(d3-067)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Second Order Derivatives 67 (d3-067)}
\textit{Difficulty: Challenge \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 8}

\question{A function is defined by \( f(x) = x^3 + px^2 + qx + 1 \), where \( p \) and \( q \) are constants. The curve \( y = f(x) \) has a stationary point at \( (2, -3) \). (a) Find \( p \) and \( q \). (b) Determine the nature of the stationary point.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Use \( f(2) = -3 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
8 + 4p + 2q + 1 = -3 \;\Rightarrow\; 4p + 2q = -12 \;\Rightarrow\; 2p + q = -6
\end{gathered}\]
First equation.

\textit{Step 2: Use \( f'(2) = 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 3x^2 + 2px + q \;\Rightarrow\; f'(2) = 12 + 4p + q = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Stationary point condition.

\textit{Step 3: Solve the simultaneous equations.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\begin{cases} 2p + q = -6 \\ 4p + q = -12 \end{cases} \;\Rightarrow\; 2p = -6 \;\Rightarrow\; p = -3, \; q = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Subtract the first from the second.

\textit{Step 4: (b) Second derivative.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f''(x) = 6x + 2p = 6x - 6 \\ f''(2) = 12 - 6 = 6 > 0
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \(p = -3\) into \( f''(x) = 6x + 2p \), then evaluate at \(x = 2\). A positive value means concave up --- a local minimum.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  p = -3,\ q = 0 \). \\[3pt]  (b) The stationary point is a minimum.
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Second Order Derivatives 68\quad\small\texttt{(d3-068)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Second Order Derivatives 68 (d3-068)}
\textit{Difficulty: Challenge \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 10}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = x^4 - 4x^3 + 4x^2 \). (a) Find \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} \) and \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}^2y}{\mathrm{d}x^2} \). (b) Find the stationary points and classify them. (c) Find the coordinates of any points of inflection.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Differentiate twice.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4x^3 - 12x^2 + 8x, \quad \dfrac{\mathrm{d}^2y}{\mathrm{d}x^2} = 12x^2 - 24x + 8
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: (b) Factorise and solve \( y' = 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
4x(x^2 - 3x + 2) = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; 4x(x-1)(x-2) = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Three solutions: \( x = 0, 1, 2 \).

\textit{Step 3: Find \( y \) values.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y(0) = 0, \quad y(1) = 1 - 4 + 4 = 1, \quad y(2) = 16 - 32 + 16 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute.

\textit{Step 4: Apply the second derivative test.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y''(0) = 8 > 0 \; (\text{min}) \\ y''(1) = 12 - 24 + 8 = -4 < 0 \; (\text{max}) \\ y''(2) = 48 - 48 + 8 = 8 > 0 \; (\text{min})
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute each stationary point into the second derivative. Positive values are local minima (concave up); the negative value at \(x=1\) is a local maximum (concave down).

\textit{Step 5: (c) Solve \( y'' = 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
12x^2 - 24x + 8 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; 3x^2 - 6x + 2 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x = \dfrac{6 \pm \sqrt{12}}{6} = 1 \pm \dfrac{\sqrt{3}}{3}
\end{gathered}\]
Use the quadratic formula.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  y' = 4x^3 - 12x^2 + 8x \), \(\displaystyle  y'' = 12x^2 - 24x + 8 \) \\[3pt]  (b) Minima at \(\displaystyle  (0, 0) \) and \(\displaystyle  (2, 0) \); maximum at \(\displaystyle  (1, 1) \) \\[3pt]  (c) Inflection at \(\displaystyle  x = 1 \pm \tfrac{\sqrt{3}}{3} \).
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Second Order Derivatives 69\quad\small\texttt{(d3-069)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Second Order Derivatives 69 (d3-069)}
\textit{Difficulty: Challenge \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 9}

\question{An open rectangular tank has a square base of side \( x \) metres and height \( h \) metres. Its volume is \( 32 \) m\textsuperscript{3}. (a) Show that its external surface area is \( S = x^2 + \dfrac{128}{x} \). (b) Find the value of \( x \) that minimises \( S \), using the second derivative to confirm it is a minimum.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Volume condition.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x^2 h = 32 \;\Rightarrow\; h = \dfrac{32}{x^2}
\end{gathered}\]
Solve for \( h \).

\textit{Step 2: Surface area (base + four sides, open top).}
\[\begin{gathered}
S = x^2 + 4xh = x^2 + 4x \cdot \dfrac{32}{x^2} = x^2 + \dfrac{128}{x} \;\checkmark
\end{gathered}\]
As required.

\textit{Step 3: (b) Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
S = x^2 + 128 x^{-1} \;\Rightarrow\; \dfrac{\mathrm{d}S}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - 128 x^{-2}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 4: Set to zero.}
\[\begin{gathered}
2x = \dfrac{128}{x^2} \;\Rightarrow\; x^3 = 64 \;\Rightarrow\; x = 4
\end{gathered}\]
Solve.

\textit{Step 5: Second derivative.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}^2S}{\mathrm{d}x^2} = 2 + 256 x^{-3}
\end{gathered}\]
Differentiate once more.

\textit{Step 6: Evaluate at \( x = 4 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
2 + \dfrac{256}{64} = 2 + 4 = 6 > 0 \; \Rightarrow \; \text{minimum}
\end{gathered}\]
The second derivative is positive at \(x = 4\), so the surface area function is concave up there. This confirms a local minimum.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) Shown. \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  x = 4 \) m gives the minimum surface area.
\end{answerbox}
\textbf{Diagram.}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Second Order Derivatives 70\quad\small\texttt{(d3-070)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Second Order Derivatives 70 (d3-070)}
\textit{Difficulty: Challenge \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 10}

\question{A function is defined by \( f(x) = 2x^3 - 15x^2 + 24x + 7 \). (a) Find \( f'(x) \) and \( f''(x) \). (b) Find the coordinates of the stationary points. (c) Use the second derivative test to determine the nature of each. (d) State the range of values of \( x \) for which \( f(x) \) is concave up.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 6x^2 - 30x + 24, \quad f''(x) = 12x - 30
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: (b) Solve \( f'(x) = 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
6(x^2 - 5x + 4) = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; (x-1)(x-4) = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Factorise.

\textit{Step 3: Find \( y \) values.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(1) = 2 - 15 + 24 + 7 = 18 \\ f(4) = 128 - 240 + 96 + 7 = -9
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute.

\textit{Step 4: (c) Second derivative test.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f''(1) = 12 - 30 = -18 < 0 \; (\text{max}) \\ f''(4) = 48 - 30 = 18 > 0 \; (\text{min})
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute each stationary point into \( f''(x) \). Negative \(\Rightarrow\) concave down (local maximum); positive \(\Rightarrow\) concave up (local minimum).

\textit{Step 5: (d) Concave up where \( f''(x) > 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
12x - 30 > 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x > \tfrac{5}{2}
\end{gathered}\]
A curve is concave up exactly where its second derivative is positive. Solve the linear inequality directly --- no sign flip needed.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = 6x^2 - 30x + 24 \), \(\displaystyle  f''(x) = 12x - 30 \) \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  (1, 18) \) and \(\displaystyle  (4, -9) \) \\[3pt]  (c) Maximum at \(\displaystyle  (1, 18) \); minimum at \(\displaystyle  (4, -9) \) \\[3pt]  (d) Concave up for \(\displaystyle  x > \tfrac{5}{2} \).
\end{answerbox}
